### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.07.08  
하반기 기대해주세요

2Q26 Preview 
기아는 2Q26E 매출액 32.4조원(+10.5% YoY, +9.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.7조원(-
1.3% YoY, +23.8% QoQ, OPM 8.4%)을 기록할 것으로 예상, 컨센서스에 부합할 전
망이다. 
물량 증가에 믹스 개선까지 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.9만대(+4.3% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+4.5%)를 기록했다. 판매 효과는 YoY 1,500억 증가했을 것으로 추정된다. 생산 차
질에도 불구하고, 기아의 경우 EV3/EV5 등 신차 효과와 국내 전기차 보조금 확대로 
EV 국내 판매량은 전년 동기 대비 123% 증가했고 유럽 역시 EV 판매량이 1.5만대 
증가했다. HEV 판매 증가세(+50.2%)는 여전히 견조한 상황이다. 미국 내 2분기 텔루
라이드 판매량 3.7만대 중 HEV 판매는 2.0만대로, 2세대 판매 본격화로 믹스 개선은 
점차 강해질 것이다. 텔루라이드 판매 목표(18만 대) 달성 시, 손익 효과는 +0.9조원
일 것으로 예상된다. 
투자의견 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 240,000원으로 상향 
판매량 전망치 상향과 달러 강세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 상향하며, 이에 따라 목
표주가를 상향한다. 동사는 북미/유럽에서 친환경차 확대를 통한 실적 성장이 나타나
고 있다. 경쟁사 대비 펀더멘털이 견고한 동사의 밸류에이션 재평가가 나타날 것으로 
예상된다. 동사 역시 그룹사 로보틱스 핵심 자산에 대한 동일한 접근과 지분을 확보, 
향후 로봇 생산 법인(RA)와 RMAC 설립 시에도, 기아 역시 대규모 지분 투자가 이루
어질 것이다. 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.3배)보
다 낮은 수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 190: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로삼성바이오로직스(207940) 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 - 기업.pdf

기업분석 2026.07.21 www.daishin.com

Issue Comment 
삼성바이오

(207940) 
 항체를 넘어 펩타이드로 
홍가혜

kahye0.hong@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 2,000,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.07.20) 
1,345,000 
제약업종

◼ 2.7조원 규모의 폴리펩타이드 인수로 펩타이드 CDMO 진출 
◼ 차세대 모달리티 경쟁력 강화 및 글로벌 생산거점 확보 
◼ 중장기 전략에는 긍정적, 거래 종결 및 통합 과정 확인 필요

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스는 7/20 공시를 통해 약 2.7조원 규모의 스위스 펩타이드 
CDMO 업체 폴리펩타이드 그룹(SIX: PPGN) 인수를 발표함. 이번 인수를 통
해 기존 항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 모달리티를 확대하고, 미국▪유럽▪
인도 생산거점을 확보했다는 점에서 중장기 성장 전략에 부합하는 긍정적인 
이벤트로 판단함. 특히 GLP-1 기반 비만치료제 시장 성장으로 수요가 확대
되는 펩타이드 시장에 M&A를 통해 즉시 진입해 자체 증설 대비 시장 진입 
기간을 단축했다는 점도 긍정적임. 다만, 거래 종결 이후 통합 시너지의 구체
화가 필요한 것으로 판단되어 목표주가에는 미반영함.

폴리펩타이드 그룹은 글로벌 Top-tier 펩타이드 CDMO 
폴리펩타이드 그룹은 약 70년 업력의 펩타이드 전문 CDMO로, 미국▪유럽▪인
도 등 5개국에서 6개의 cGMP 생산거점을 운영 중임. 1,000개 이상의 펩타이
드 의약품 생산 경험과 글로벌 임상3상 펩타이드 파이프라인의 약 1/3을 확
보하고 있어, 향후 상업화 전환에 따른 생산 물량 증가가 기대됨. ‘25년 매출
은 3.89억 유로를 기록했으며, 대사질환 매출 비중이 빠르게 확대 중임(‘21년 
22%→’25년 57%). ‘26년 매출 성장률 20-25%, EBITDA 마진 mid-to-high 
teens를 가이던스로 제시함.

전략적 의미: 모달리티+생산거점 확대를 통한 성장동력 다변화 
이번 인수의 핵심은 펩타이드 모달리티와 글로벌 생산거점을 동시에 확보했
다는 것임. GLP-1 중심의 고성장 시장에 즉시 진입하는 동시에 기존 생산시
설과 고객 기반을 승계해 인수 직후 매출 기여가 가능하며, 미국▪유럽▪인도 
거점을 기반으로 공급망 경쟁력도 강화될 전망임. 또한 양사가 보유한 글로벌 
고객 기반을 활용한 교차 수주와 운영 효율화를 통한 중장기 수익성 개선도 
기대됨.

중장기 전략에는 긍정적, 거래 종결 및 통합 과정 확인 필요 
이번 인수는 연내 마무리를 목표로 하며, 공개매수는 9월 개시 이후 약 2개
월간 진행될 예정임. 최대주주의 공개매수 응모 확약으로 거래 성사 가능성은 
높은 것으로 판단함. 인수자금은 보유 현금과 영업현금흐름으로 상당 부분 충
당 가능할 전망임. 다만, 6공장 및 송도 제3캠퍼스 투자 등 병행되는 대규모 
CAPEX를 감안하면 추가 차입 가능성은 존재하며, 향후 거래 종결과 자금조
달 방식은 확인이 필요함.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Is AI a bubble? The extraordinary valuations and scale
of investment raise the question. Missing, though, is the
speculative behaviour of buying an asset now with the
intent of resale for profit later.
• It is natural to want to look for parallels in history. Neither

the late 1990s/early 2000s ‘dot com’ nor the 19th century
railway booms quite match the current episode. Better
analogies may be found in other price cycles stemming from
investment booms, spurred by structural shifts. In the rush
to build for the new technology, construction costs and
end-product prices rise before eventually correcting. Time-
to-build and resource constraints can create price cycles
even where classic speculative behaviour is absent.

• Non-financial goals around geopolitics or a rush to build a
god-like intelligence could blind investors to commercial
reality and encourage investments that do not pay off. To
avoid a mad flap that takes us, Icarus-like, too close to the
sun, choices must be based on cost and ROI.
The exceptional valuations on AI-adjacent firms are matched
only by the extraordinary scale of capital expenditure on
data centres, both overseas and in Australia. The use of

leverage and sometimes opaque financing structures twitch
the antennae of those of us who have been through financial
boom–bust cycles before. Naturally, people question the
return on investment and whether this is a “bubble”. But is it?
It helps to define terms. The usual definition of a bubble is a
deviation of asset prices from “fundamental” value, but this is
only as good as your model of fundamentals. The distinctive
feature in episodes labelled as bubbles is speculative

behaviour: investors buying an asset mainly or solely because
they expect its price will rise so they can resell at a profit.
(In technical terms, the current price of the asset depends
on expectations of future price growth). In the extreme, the
market is in “Greater Fool” territory, where investors admit that
they do not think the revenue warrants the price, but believe
they will find a Greater Fool to buy it later.
Property assets are one of the main areas where “bubbly”

boom–bust cycles can occur, because investors can use
more leverage than for other assets. The asset also does not
become obsolete quickly and much of the value is in the non-
depreciating land or location component.
Data centres are a kind of property, and leverage is clearly
being used to finance their construction. Even so, the
speculative behaviour that typically characterises property
AI-carus?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
bubbles is missing: nobody is making these investments in

expectation of reselling data centres to make a quick buck. It is
all about the expected value of the computing services these
data centres will provide. Currently the “cost of compute”
seems to be on the rise as the firms building the models
try to shift their customer bases away from free and flat-
rate subscriptions onto higher-cost pay-by-use plans. Yet
everyone knows that AI can only achieve the desired returns
by expanding usage, which means it needs to be cheaper

than the human labour it seeks to replace. Even if investment
decisions are based on expected returns on services provided
by that capital, the realism of those return expectations can
and should be scrutinised.
It is natural to want to look for parallels in history.
The seemingly obvious one is the ‘dot com’ boom of the late
1990s/early 2000s. But while there are some similarities
to that episode, there are also important differences. Back
then, the tech firms building the new technology were not

so financially intertwined with the telco firms buying the
spectrum, laying the fibre – and borrowing the money.
There are also similarities to the railway boom of the 19th
century, except that location is not as inherent to a data centre.
You can train an LLM anywhere you can access the electricity,
water and construction inputs. A railway, by contrast, goes
from somewhere to somewhere else, and the returns to
duplicating the route decline rapidly.

Better analogies to the current AI/data centre boom can be
found in other price cycles. Many boom–bust cycles start
from something real and substantial. A new technology might
instigate an equities boom. Opening up a new region might
instigate a land boom there. The value underlying the boom
remains even after the period of over-exuberance ends. We did
not regret creating the Internet, or building that fibre network,
or opening up regions through better transport.

What we did sometimes do, though, is pay too much for what
we got. If everyone is rushing to build the infrastructure,
hoping not to be the Slower Builder who ends up losing money,
pressure on construction resources rises, and so do construction
prices. We have seen similar cost surges during the mining
“Data centres do not have the same
network effects or location-based
natural monopoly characteristics."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
boom and more recently in the cycle of infrastructure projects
in the eastern seaboard states. (This is likely one reason why
suppliers of construction materials were so quick to pass
increased fuel and transport costs into their prices.)
Along the way, the cost of the services also rise, precisely

because of the lags involved in expanding the infrastructure.
There is an analogy here in things like shipping costs, which show
boom–bust price cycles even though there has obviously never
been a bubble in shipping services. In shipping, office property
and now data centres, demand might increase but supply is fixed
in the short term because it takes time to build that infrastructure.
The price of the service therefore rises in the upswing phase.

This upswing phase can also spill over into related sectors,
as seen this time in the broader tech sector. Export prices for
Korean semiconductors have exploded in the past 6–9 months,
up more than 150% over the year. Pass-through to US import
prices is more muted, given lags and the usual pricing to market,
but tech inflation has increased. As well as the much-publicised
price increases for iPads and Macs, the CPI components covering
computers and other tech goods have picked up recently in

Australia and other advanced economies, to inflation rates
close to the peak of the pandemic. This is a departure from their
usual deflationary pulse and shows that the current inflationary
environment is about more than the US–Iran conflict.
The high costs of the upswing phase nonetheless contain the
seeds of their reversal. They discourage use of the underlying
service, so adoption is slower than its boosters assume. They
also encourage further innovations to economise on the

expensive input; routing algorithms to choose the most cost-
effective AI model are one example. Over time, as data centres
are completed, the supply of computing power expands and
its price falls. Investments that only made sense when the
service’s price was high will become distressed.
Capitalism has its drawbacks, but at least raw commercial
incentives limit people’s appetite to overpay for things. When
all the focus is instead on geopolitical rivalries or being the first

to build god-like intelligence, commercial discipline is lost. A
potentially beneficial innovation wave instead risks becoming an
Icarus-like mad flap that takes us too close to the sun. That is the
perfect environment for a price cycle, including in the tech sector
more broadly.
The end result need not be a meltdown. That depends on
the debt: how much there is relative to revenues after the
price downswing, who borrowed it and who lent it. The role

of deep-pocketed tech firms eases some concerns. Some
borrowers are substantial firms with other revenues. In
addition, other tech firms who have not leveraged themselves
to the data centre build-out have big enough cash piles to pick
up the pieces (at discounted prices) should some projects come
unstuck. Regulators will be most concerned about whether
the lenders who lent to the hyperscalers and AI firms in turn
funded themselves from parts of the financial system that

amplify shocks through their leverage and connection to the
regulated banking system.
The answer is not to regulate AI out of existence or stymie
its further development – a point the federal government has
clearly heeded in the PM’s latest announcements. The advice
I’d give now is the same as I gave a decade ago, in a different
context: in the face of structural change giving rise to a boom,
go in with your eyes open. Don’t get ahead of yourself. And

have something to catch you when you fall.
Humans have managed to transcend Greek mythology and
master the art of flying without melting. Yet the message of
Icarus’s story endures: there are dangers when hubris goes
unchecked. To avoid getting ahead of yourself, it helps to let
commercial considerations shape decisions, especially around
borrowing. Far better that than basing decisions on imagined
scenarios that do not survive contact with real-world economics.

“What worries me about the current
boom is how far financial considerations
seem to sit in the background"

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: frayed nerves
0.2%mth, and the prior month's gain was revised lower from
0.4% to 0.1%. The latest readings on consumer demand are
also consistent with inflation returning to target, nominal
control group retail sales up a modest 0.5% in June after a

0.8% gain in May, and the latest Beige Book also pointing
to consumer demand being constrained by cost-of-living
pressures. The duration and severity of the renewed conflict in
the Middle East will be critical to the US growth outlook given
University of Michigan consumer sentiment remains stuck near
multi-decade lows.
Over in Asia, China's Q2 GDP and June activity data provided
a mixed read on their economy. Q2 GDP met expectations at
0.9%qtr, although the annual rate disappointed at 4.3%yr.

Year-to-date, growth remains broadly consistent with our
expectation of 4.7%, but such an outcome would be at the lower
end of authorities' 4.5–5.0% full-year target. The industrial
sector continues to drive aggregate momentum, production
growth rebounding to 5.3%yr in June from around 4.0%yr
through April and May. But the domestic growth story remains
fragile, primarily owing to declining investment, -5.7%ytd in
June. Retail sales growth recovered from -0.6%yr in May to

1.0%yr in June, though that still leaves it at the lower end of the
historical rage. Together, the state of domestic investment and
household consumption call for urgent pro-active stimulus to
support cyclical momentum as well as structural change to help
the gains of trade flow to the wider economy.
The Bank of Korea raised rates by 25bp this week and
signalled further tightening is likely. Inflation remains elevated,
due to a combination of higher energy, exchange rate and food

costs alongside resilient domestic demand. But the BoK's
greater challenge is managing an increasingly two-speed
economy, with tech-related sectors benefiting directly from
the AI and data centre investment boom while others wait
for those gains to spill over. For now, policymakers appear
comfortable leaning against inflation and expecting the
benefits of AI production and investment to broaden; although,
in the interim, this leaves Korea exposed should enthusiasm

around AI turn. Investment and the implications for growth
and policy is a focus of this month's edition of The Wrap on
Asia; and, with a particular focus on AI investment, Chief
Economist Luci Ellis’ weekly essay .
The Westpac–MI Consumer Sentiment Index rose 4.1% in July;
but at 83.9, the index is still in the bottom decile of historical
outcomes, pointing to deep pessimism. Lower fuel prices have
provided some breathing room, contributing to a less-negative

assessment of ‘family finances vs a year ago’ (+5.6%) and ‘family
finances next 12mths’ (+13.4%). Consumers remain sensitive
to developments in the Middle East, however, last week’s re-
escalation of the conflict resulting in a progressive deterioration
in daily responses while the survey was in the field.
Views on the economic outlook are weak and little changed,
the one- and five-years ahead sub-indexes lifting just 0.6%
and 0.7% in the month; more encouragingly, concerns over

the jobs outlook have eased. Most consumers still expect
mortgage rates to rise over the next year, though the share has
declined from 66% to 60%, with a rising proportion reporting
uncertainty about where rates are headed. Ultimately, the
survey reported a reprieve from worst-case fears but is also
evidence of the scale of challenges ahead.
The latest NAB business survey  echoed similar themes.
Business confidence continued to retrace the sharp falls seen

at the onset of the Middle East conflict, rising +9pts to a
less-pessimistic reading of –5. Lower fuel prices contributed
to slower growth in purchase costs and final prices, but the
cumulative impact of these pressures have weighted on capital
expenditure plans and labour demand. Business conditions
remain subdued, holding at +3 for a third consecutive month
having weakened earlier in 2026.
Offshore, US inflation data was supportive of interest rates

remaining on hold in coming months. Headline prices fell 0.4%
in June as energy prices responded to the Middle East ceasefire
agreed mid-month, pulling annual inflation down from 4.2%yr
to 3.5%yr. Of greater significance for monetary policy, however,
was the flat monthly read for core inflation, 2.6%yr.
Members of the FOMC want to see further evidence of
disinflation towards the 2.0%yr target. In our view, the multi-
month trends are consistent with such an out-turn, with

core goods prices broadly unchanged year-to-date and core
services inflation averaging just below 0.3% per month. The
June PPI report corroborates this view, headline producer
prices falling 0.3%, while May was revised down sharply
from 1.1% to 0.6%. Ex food and energy, prices rose a modest

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Soggy activity data ahead of key CPI report
Following last week’s RBNZ meeting, which resulted in
the first OCR hike in three years, the market has turned its
attention to the prospect of a follow-up rate hike at the
next meeting on 2 September. The past week has provided

numerous economic reports and a key RBNZ speech for
the market to digest, covering topics such as business
sentiment, consumer spending, housing, the labour market,
tourism and inflation.
Early in the week, the QSBO – New Zealand’s premier business
survey – pointed to a modest lift in business sentiment in the
June quarter. The survey period ran from 10 June to 7 July. The
average response over that period meant that the headline
confidence index rose 11pts to +12. Early responses were a

net 5% negative, while the second major batch of responses
– mostly arriving after the signing of the Memorandum of
Understanding by the US and Iran – were a net 20% positive.
Given the renewed hostilities in the Middle East this month,
the earlier responses may be more representative of where
sentiment would stand if the survey was re-run today. Firms’
expectations of their own activity, which tend to have a closer
correspondence with GDP growth, were little changed for the

quarter. Other activity indicators were mixed. Investment and
hiring intentions were less negative than in the March quarter,
but profitability was expected to worsen further. Meanwhile,
the rise in fuel prices since March was clearly reflected in the
pricing measures. A net 41% of firms reported raising their
prices in the June quarter, up from 22% last quarter and the
highest reading since September 2023. In the next three
months, a net 54% of firms indicated an intention to raise their

prices – the highest since March 2023.
News from the retail sector was more downbeat than expected
in June, with the value of retail spending processed through the
electronic payments system declining 1.4%, reversing the step
higher seen in May. Of note was a 4% decline in spending on
household durables, while spending on apparel and hospitality
both fell 2%. The only category that saw a rise in June was
spending on groceries. Given the June result, total spending

over the June quarter increased just 0.4%, driven entirely by
a 7.3% lift in spending on fuel. Core retail spending fell 0.1%,
marking the first decline since the March quarter last year.
Our own recent survey of households confirmed that
consumers remain cautious. In response to developments
in the Middle East, 36% of respondents reported having
wound back their discretionary spending and around 15%
have delayed or cancelled travel plans. Even when it comes to

spending on essentials like groceries, around 30% said they’ve
changed how they shop e.g., by purchasing fewer or cheaper
items. Looking ahead, the pressure on fuel costs looks likely to
persist, especially given the rebound in oil prices this week due
to renewed tensions in the Middle East. Combined with other
cost of living pressures, a soft labour market and continued
economic uncertainty, we expect growth in consumer spending
will remain comparatively subdued.

The housing market data also struck a soggy note in June,
with house sales edging lower and so added to a cumulative
9% decline since the start of the year. The median time to sell
was little changed and so remained well above the historic
average. Reflecting buyers’ lack of urgency, the REINZ house
price index fell by 0.3% for the month and was down 0.8% on
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
a year ago. National average prices have moved sideways for
the last three years, though with some marked distinctions
across regions. Wellington and Auckland have seen the
largest price declines over the last year, while regions with
stronger links to agriculture and tourism, such as Canterbury

and Otago, have seen prices continue to set new highs.
Fixed-term mortgage rates have risen since the start of this
year, in anticipation of the next RBNZ tightening cycle (which
is now in motion). Looking ahead, we expect that higher
interest rates will continue to weigh on the housing market,
leaning against any improvement in incomes as the economy
gets back on its feet. Meanwhile, demand for housing remains
constrained by relatively weak population growth. New

Zealand saw a net inflow of just 1,860 migrants in May, with
a downward revision to earlier months reducing the annual
inflow to less than 19,000. Given historically weak natural
growth in the population due to a declining fertility rate,
annual population growth is running at less than 1%.
In some better news, MBIE’s measure of job advertising picked
up in June, perhaps helped by – at the time – a recovery in
business confidence amidst easing tensions in the Middle East.

The level of advertising remains low by historical standards
but is now 13% higher than a year earlier. Not surprisingly, the
strongest lift in job vacancies was seen in the primary sector,
reflecting buoyant prices for almost all of New Zealand’s key
exported commodities. This is also reflected in the regional
breakdown, with the major urban area of Auckland yet to see
any lift in vacancies from the cycle low point.
Moving on, tourist data for May suggests some impact from the

Middle East conflict with non-resident arrivals declining 2.4%
to be 3.6% below their recent February peak. Even so, arrivals
were around 7% higher than a year earlier reflecting the strong
growth seen since around the middle of last year. Much of that
growth owes to arrivals from Australia – New Zealand’s largest
market – and Asia, including a significant uplift in arrivals from
China (the latter up more than 30% from a year earlier). By
contrast, arrivals from major North American and European

markets were lower than a year earlier. Looking ahead, we
continue to expect a soft patch in tourism arrivals over the
second half of this year due to the impact of high airfares and
Middle East uncertainty on long haul forward bookings.
On balance, today’s Selected Prices data for the June month
– covering around 47% of the CPI – provided a modest upside
surprise. Fuel prices – especially diesel – were lower, as
expected. Food prices posted their expected seasonal increase

while rental inflation remained very subdued. Electricity prices
rose by less than expected, but the very volatile international
accommodation component rose a much stronger-than-
expected 9% during the month. Given this information, we have
nudged up our earlier forecast and now think that next week’s
June quarter CPI report will reveal a 1.5% lift in the headline
index, raising annual inflation to 4.1% from 3.1% at present. We
view the risks around this forecast as balanced. Our estimate of

annual inflation is 0.2ppt above the updated estimated provided
by the RBNZ at the July Monetary Policy Review.
Finally, this week the RBNZ’s Chief Economist, Paul Conway,
gave a speech on pricing behaviour. He emphasised that the key
monetary policy challenge facing policymakers is to prevent
the oil shock from becoming embedded in expectations and
business pricing behaviour. In that regard, he noted several
findings from recent research – some of which is ongoing at the

Bank – which in his view raise the risk of inflation persistence:
• Businesses and households pay much more attention to
inflation when it is above the 1-3% target range, making
expectations more sensitive to current inflation outcomes.
• Household inflation expectations have risen and become
more dispersed since the Middle East conflict, indicating
greater uncertainty about future inflation.
• Firms now change prices more frequently than in the past

due to lower repricing costs and digital technology.
• Businesses are more willing to pass through cost increases
than they are to reverse price rises when costs fall, creating a
greater risk that temporary shocks become lasting inflation.
• These effects appear particularly strong in the services
sector, where inflation tends to be more persistent.
Conway noted that the RBNZ may need to respond more
forcefully to re-anchor expectations if businesses and

households begin to expect inflation to remain elevated.
Balanced against this, he argued – correctly in our view – that
the current situation differs markedly from the inflationary
episodes of the 1970s and from the post-pandemic inflation
surge. In particular, the New Zealand economy currently has
spare capacity, which should make it harder for firms to raise
prices aggressively and should help inflation return to target
over time. Medium-term inflation expectations also remain

relatively well anchored.
Nonetheless, the market interpreted the speech as “hawkish”,
especially when set against the dovish comments that Conway
had previously made in recent months. Combined with
developments in the Middle East and elsewhere, the speech
has consolidated the market’s confidence that the RBNZ will
deliver a further lift in the OCR at the 2 September meeting,
with a 25bp hike now around 90% priced.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Jun Westpac-MI Leading Index (%ann'd)
Jul 22, Last: –0.17
The Leading Index growth rate ticked up slightly to –0.17%
in May, the read still consistent with sluggish, below-trend
growth momentum through the second half of 2026 and into
early 2027.

The June read will include a mixed bunch of component
updates. On the plus side, the ASX200 lifted 0.5% and
consumer sentiment-based measures posted some recovery.
However, there were a significant weakening across several
other components including: commodity prices (–0.7% in
AUD terms); dwelling approvals (–1.1%); total hours worked
(–1.1%); and a further narrowing in the yield spread. That looks
likely to see some further softening in growth momentum

overall.
AUS: Jun Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Jul 23, Last: 40.3, Westpac f/c: 15
Market f/c: 15, Range: flat to 50
After expanding around +26k/mth in Q1, employment growth
effectively stalled over April and May. On a three-month
average basis, employment is growing well below the pace
of the working-age population, leaving the employment-to-
population ratio 0.7ppts below its recent historical high.
The brief recovery around the start of the year has well and

truly faded. Higher inflation and recent interest rate rises
will still take time to fully work its way through to the labour
market, but for now, it is looking weaker than earlier in the year.
Forward-looking business survey measures point to sluggish
employment growth ahead. We have pencilled in a +15k lift in
June, and see risks tilted to the downside. See our full preview.
AUS: Jun Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Jul 23, Last: 4.4, Westpac f/c: 4.4

Market f/c: 4.4, Range: 4.3 to 4.5
The unemployment rate also eased as expected, edging down
from a cycle high 4.5% to 4.4% in May as various sources
of noise faded. Given the participation rate has held up a bit
better than employment, the trend for the unemployment rate
has been a continued grind higher.
For June, we think both the participation and unemployment
rates will hold steady. But as discussed in our preview,
re-published official data and new unofficial measures are

pointing to a material increase in underemployment in recent
months. Both demand-side (weaker employment) and supply-
side (cost-of-living pressures) are likely at play, and it may be
pointing to higher measured unemployment down the track.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q2 Consumer Price Index (%)
Jul 21, Quarterly, Last: 0.9, Westpac f/c: 1.5
Annual, Last: 3.1%, Westpac f/c: 4.1, RBNZ f/c: 3.9
With the war in the Middle East pushing oil prices sharply
higher, consumer price inflation is set to rise to a two-year

high. We estimate that New Zealand consumer prices rose by
1.5% in the June quarter. The annual inflation rate is expected
to rise to 4.1% (up from 3.1% previously). That’s slightly higher
than the 3.9% the RBNZ assumed in their recent July update,
however much of the surprise is likely related to volatile items
like holiday accommodation. More importantly, core inflation
has been softening but remains above the RBNZ’s 2% target
midpoint. That’s despite the downturn in economic growth and

softness in the labour market.

ngay từ phiên đầu tuần sau thông tin căng thẳng Mỹ - Iran bùng phát trở lại, khiến giá dầu 
bật tăng mạnh và đảo chiều các kỳ vọng về thỏa thuận hòa bình trước đó. Bên cạnh đó, 
việc mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao, và thông tin tiêu cực về doanh nghiệp đầu ngành 
bán lẻ trang sức PNJ bị điều tra cũng gây áp lực lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Mùa báo cáo KQKD 
quý 2 đang diễn ra với nhiều điểm sáng về tăng trưởng, tuy nhiên không đủ nâng đỡ thị 
trường chung.

17/07/2026

Triển vọng kinh tế 2H2026 
Chủ động thích ứng, củng cố tăng trưởng

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Chúng tôi dự báo tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 đạt 8.0 - 8.5%, với động lực 
tăng trưởng chính đến từ kỳ vọng Chính phủ đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư 
công, dòng vốn FDI đầu tư vào Việt Nam duy trì xu hướng tích cực và khu 
vực sản xuất công nghiệp tăng trưởng tốt theo đà phục hồi của hoạt động 
xuất nhập khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng 2H2026, Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục 
đối mặt với một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm xung đột Mỹ – 
Iran có thể kéo dài hơn dự kiến, nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI cùng những 
căng thẳng Trung Quốc – Đài Loan…

2) Chúng tôi hạ dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam về 
quanh mức 4.5% YoY tương đương mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của 
Chính phủ, dựa trên diễn biến khả quan của tình hình địa chính trị và giá 
năng lượng thế giới trong 2H2026.

3) Chúng tôi đánh giá áp lực tỷ giá USD/VND trong 2H2026 sẽ tương đối ổn 
định, với dự báo tăng 1.5 - 2% trong năm 2026 chủ yếu nhờ chênh lệch 
dương lãi suất VND-USD, nguồn cung ngoại tệ dồi dào từ nâng hạng thị 
trường và dòng FDI công nghệ cao.

4) Chúng tôi cho rằng lãi suất huy động đã đang ở vùng đỉnh, sẽ tiếp tục neo 
cao trong quý 3, và k ỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ vào đầu quý 4 nhờ áp lực tỷ 
giá - lạm phát giảm, các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản/hạ lãi suất từ NHNN, 
nhu cầu nắm giữ tiền mặt hạ nhiệt và nguồn tiền từ đầu tư công được giải 
phóng vào hệ thống.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành một cây nến đỏ giảm điểm thân dài trên khung tuần cho thấy áp lực 
phân phối đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, chỉ số cũng trải qua xu hướng giảm điểm, 
bất chấp 2 phiên hồi phục đan xen. Điểm đáng chú ý là tín hiệu hồi phục chỉ tập trung ở 
một số ít các mã trong khi số đông còn lại vẫn đang nằm trong nhịp điều chỉnh và điều này 
hàm ý dòng tiền về tổng thể vẫn đang dần bị thu hẹp. Mặc dù vậy, mặt tích cực là lực cầu 
tham gia bắt đáy ở các vùng giá thấp vẫn đang được duy trì khá ổn định, đặc biệt là khi 
VNINDEX đã quay xuống vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1760 và khá nhiều mã bluechips đã về lại các 
điểm đỡ mạnh bên dưới. Điều này gợi mở cho khả năng sớm hồi phục trở lại cho chỉ số 
trong tuần tới. 
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1890 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1850 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1760 (+-15) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

PNJ 
Cập nhật về sai phạm của công ty con PNJ - lab

Giám đốc kiểm định P-lab bị khởi tố, bắt giam vì hợp thức viên kim cương nhập lậu 
— Theo cơ quan điều tra, Giám đốc kiểm định của P-lab (công ty con do PNJ sở hữu 100% 
vốn) đã cấu kết với đường dây buôn lậu bên ngoài, lợi dụng thẩm quyền và uy tín của 
P-lab để cấp giấy chứng nhận chất lượng, hợp thức hóa nguồn gốc cho khoảng 28,000 
viên kim cương nhập lậu trước khi tung ra thị trường.  
— Về P-lab, đây là đơn vị kiểm định trang sức, đá quý độc lập, cung cấp dịch vụ cho PNJ, 
khách hàng cá nhân và các bên thứ ba, đạt chuẩn quốc tế (ISO 17025) với hệ thống máy 
móc hiện đại. Ban điều hành P-lab vận hành độc lập về chuyên môn, không phụ thuộc 
trực tiếp vào công ty mẹ, nhưng vẫn chịu sự kiểm soát nội bộ và quản trị rủi ro của PNJ. 
Sau sự việc, HĐQT đã bổ sung 2 thành viên độc lập đại diện giám sát tại P-lab. 
— Phản ứng thị trường: lượng khách bán lại sản phẩm kim cương của công ty tăng cao 
trong các ngày đầu sau sự việc, nhưng đã giảm dần và về mức bình thường sau khoảng 
4 ngày tính đến sáng nay. PNJ cho biết các khách hàng vẫn tiếp tục mua hoặc đổi sang 
sản phẩm khác, cho thấy niềm tin thương hiệu chưa bị ảnh hưởng nghiêm trọng.

PNJ chủ động ứng phó khủng hoảng, khẳng định rủi ro không lan sang hoạt động kinh 
doanh chính 
— Về hoạt động mua lại và tồn kho: lượng kim cương khách hàng bán lại làm tăng tồn kho 
tạm thời dù không nhiều, nhưng PNJ dự kiến sẽ bán ra trong các mùa kinh doanh khác 
nên kỳ vọng không ảnh hưởng nhiều. Tỉ trọng mảng kim cương thô trong doanh 
thu/tồn kho của công ty ước tính khá nhỏ so với mảng trang sức (mảng sản phẩm thiết 
kế có sử dụng nhiều loại kim loại khác nhau như vàng, bạc, kim cương và đá quý). 
— Về quy trình nội bộ: PNJ đã rà soát toàn diện quy trình tại P-lab, đang xem xét mời 
chuyên gia độc lập đánh giá như bên thứ ba, đồng thời lập tổ công tác đặc biệt xử lý 
khủng hoảng và chủ động theo dõi truyền thông (social listening) để kịp thời giải đáp 
thắc mắc từ khách hàng và thị trường. 
— Về mức độ liên quan đến hoạt động kinh doanh chính: Chủ tịch HĐQT khẳng định PNJ 
không mua kim cương từ nhà cung cấp trong nước mà chỉ nhập khẩu kim cương thô 
chính ngạch từ Thái Lan và Hồng Kông, có đầy đủ hóa đơn, chứng từ kiểm định; P-lab 
chỉ ký hợp đồng dịch vụ kiểm định với PNJ, không phát sinh giao dịch mua bán sản 
phẩm, nên lô kim cương vi phạm không đi vào hệ thống PNJ.

KQKD sơ bộ Q2/2026 vẫn bám sát kế hoạch, BLĐ và nội bộ cân nhắc đăng ký mua 
— Sơ bộ, BLĐ cho biết doanh thu và LNST Q2/2026 vẫn tăng trưởng hai chữ số so với cùng 
kỳ, với mảng bán lẻ trang sức tăng trưởng dương và biên lợi nhuận gộp giữ theo kế 
hoạch.

## Document 257: Dutch households weather higher energy prices as income growth cushions impact

Source PDF: all report pdf/Dutch households weather higher energy prices as income growth cushions impact.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 23 July 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  23 July 2026   THE NETHERLANDS
Dutch households weather higher energy
prices as income growth cushions
impact
ING household data for the Dutch economy shows that average
spending on fuel and energy is now higher than in 2022. But as incomes
have risen substantially, the share of income spent on fuel and energy is
still significantly lower than it was four years ago
The rise in energy prices has not led to a broad-based deterioration in affordability, including among the

lowest-income households.
The Middle East crisis has not triggered a repeat of 2022
Since the outbreak of the Middle East conflict at the end of February, energy prices have
remained elevated. This has been particularly evident in the oil market, where prices have far
exceeded 2025 levels due to the closure of the Strait of Hormuz. Natural gas prices, which are
also important for electricity costs, have increased as well, but have remained closer to levels

seen in previous years.
Initial concerns about a new affordability crisis were significant, but have eased as oil prices
retreated from their peak levels. To assess the impact on households, we looked at new ING
data on spending patterns. While the share of household income spent on fuel and energy has

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 2
increased, it remains below the levels seen during the 2022 energy crisis through June.
As chart 1 shows, the average amount spent on fuel and energy remains historically high.
However, for the households in our sample, most of the increase in energy bills occurred in
2023 and 2024. The current crisis has not yet had a significant impact. This is not surprising
given the delayed pass-through to household energy contracts and the relatively muted rise in

gas prices. Fuel spending is still near the highs seen in 2022. This is consistent with fuel prices
responding very quickly to changes in oil prices and oil prices responding more significantly to
the closure of the Strait of Hormuz.
The nominal amount spent on fuel and energy is at or close to
a peak
Average monthly spending per household in euro, seasonally-adjusted
Source: ING transaction data, calculations ING Research
Household energy costs have been stable, while fuel has

reacted more quickly
As a share of income, energy bills have remained fairly steady so far this year, accounting for
around 3.4% in our sample. Fuel spending has shown a more noticeable increase, rising from
2.7% of income in December to 3.1% in April. Since then, however, that share has started to
come down as oil prices have fallen from their peak and household incomes have increased.
Overall, the impact of the Middle East war on the most directly affected spending categories

has therefore been muted. In June, the average amount of income spent on fuel and energy
was 6.1%. That is back to where it was in December of last year and down from 6.4% in April.
Income spent on energy and fuel therefore remains far from the 2022 levels of well above 7%.

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 3
Of course, there are no guarantees for the months ahead. Household energy bills adjust with a
lag and could therefore still rise as a share of income. But given current natural gas prices, we
are far from expecting a return to the levels seen in 2022. While oil and gas prices have moved
up substantially since the Middle East war reignited, both remain significantly below the peaks
seen this spring. That should temper any further increase in household energy costs in the

coming months, although the global environment remains highly uncertain.
As share of household income, monthly spending on fuel and
energy has not increased materially
Average monthly spending per household on energy and fuel*, as % of income, seasonally-
adjusted
Source: ING transaction data, calculations ING Research.
* Net payments to energy suppliers and spending at fuel stations
No broad-based deterioration across income groups
We find little evidence of a material deterioration in affordability across income groups. For the

lowest 10% of incomes in our sample, fuel and energy spending remains higher in absolute
terms, but the share of income devoted to these costs has changed little. Some income groups
have seen more pronounced changes, mostly related to fuel spending. But overall, the data
does not point to a broad-based deterioration in affordability. All income groups now spend
less as part of their income on energy and fuel than in 2022 before the energy subsidies kicked

in.

### Page 4

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 July 2026 4
Little difference in spending on fuel and energy between
income groups
Monthly spending on energy and fuel* as % of household income, average per income decile,
seasonally-adjusted
Source: ING transaction data, calculations ING Research.
* Sum of net payments to energy suppliers and spending at fuel stations
Confidence has weakened more than actual spending
What this means for the Dutch economy is that while consumer confidence has been

bouncing around significantly in response to the conflict in the Middle East and concerns about
higher inflation, spending in the categories most exposed to energy prices has not changed
materially as a share of household income. This is also true for the lowest-income decile in our
sample. This finding is consistent with general spending patterns, which have remained
resilient despite the large shock to consumer confidence.
So while the flare-up of the conflict in the Middle East has caused energy prices to spike again,

aggregated transaction data shows that, on average, household finances remain quite
resilient. We expect Dutch consumer spending growth to remain muted but still positive, as
underlying income growth supports improved spending despite higher inflation and weak
confidence.
About the data
This analysis is based on aggregated transaction data from a panel of approximately
15,000 ING customers' households aged between 20 and 80. As the transaction data is

customers. The sample was drawn using stratified sampling by province and age group
and subsequently weighted by age and income decile to better reflect the composition
of Dutch households. The analysis includes only households with income from
employment, social benefits/allowances or pensions. Self-employed households are

ソニーフィナンシャルグループ
イラン後も続く円安の正体

### Page 3

2026年7月号
中東では米国とイランの武力衝突が断続的に生じているにも
拘らず、両国による覚書と60日間の停戦合意を機に 、原油価
格は下落傾向を辿っている 。日本に影響の大きいドバイ先物
価格は65 ドル／バレルを割り込み、 中東情勢の緊迫化前で
ある本年2月初の水準に戻った。弊社も含めて、本年の第3四
半期には原油価格がもとに戻るとの見方が多かったことを考
えても、やや早い動きだ。
だからと言って、日本経済にとって、エネルギーショックによる
物価への影響が直ちに収束するわけではない。
今回、宮嶋エコノミストが 4ページで整理しているように 、米国
とイランの政治的な合意の下でもホルムズ海峡の円滑な航
行が短期間で実現する保証はない 。これまでは、湾内に滞留
していた船舶の航行再開が中心であり 、ホルムズ湾岸の原
油の生産・搬出のための施設の修復も含めて 、原油供給が3
月以前の水準を回復するには相応に時間がかかる 。さらに、
日本では、石油製品の代替調達やサプライチェーンの変更に
伴うコスト増も継続するほか、それらが消費の対象となる最終

財の価格に波及するには時間的ラグもある。
それでも 、原油価格が再び大きく反発することはないとすれ
ば、エネルギーショックの 「火元」 は鎮圧されたことになり 、
我々は、過去のショックの影響が時間をかけて弱まっていくの
を見守ることになる 。そして 、このような展開になれば、 日銀
の追加利上げは、 いくつかの点から疑問が生ずることになる 。
このうち、最もシンプルな理由は実際のインフレ率が減速する
ことである。こうした視点からの疑問は、 今後の国会で議論さ
れる食料品や外食に対する時限的な消費税の減免措置に
よって、一層拍車がかかることが想定される。
また、より丁寧な議論に基づく疑問は、 金融政策の効果の波
及には「長くて不確実な」時間的ラグがある以上、 いま利上げ
しても、効果が出現する頃にはインフレ率は既に低下しており 、
利上げには意味がないだけでなく 、経済活動を過度に抑制す
るとの懸念である。
もう一つ、日銀にとっては 、リスク評価の再検討も必要になる 。
日銀は6 月の金融政策決定会合で利上げに踏み切った際に

は、基調的物価の上昇率が 2％目標を超えて上振れるリスク
を指摘した 。その理由として 、エネルギー価格上昇の消費者
段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に繋がる可能性を挙げ
ていた。
先にみたように 、こうした現象自体は生ずるとしても 、「火元」
がなくなる以上、やがて波及は沈静化する。つまり、基調的物
価が２％目標を時限的に上回る
リスクはあるとしても 、２％目
標を超えて上振れていく リスクを主張し続けることは難しくな
る。しかも、7月の金融政策決定会合の際に改定する 「展望レ
ポート」において 、2026年度中のインフレ率見通しを4 月時点
より上方修正するようであれば 、これまでリスクと扱われてい
た内容が中心見通しに反映される結果 、残るリスクの程度は
小さくなるはずである。
つまり、現在の原油価格が本当にポスト ・エネルギーショック
の始まりを示唆する場合 、その下でも日銀が利上げを継続し
ようとすれば 、物価上昇に対するリスクマネジメント的な理由
を持ち出すことはもはや不適切になる 。その結果 、日銀は、

「物価と経済が見通しに沿って推移する限り 、金融緩和の度
合いを調整する」という元々のロジックを再び全面に押し出す
ことが必要になる。
筆者は、現在の政策金利が日銀執行部による中立金利の推
計レンジの下限にようやく達する段階にあるほか、 少なくとも
現時点で既往の利上げに伴う企業や家計、 金融市場への深
刻な影響が生じていない点を踏まえると 、日銀がさらなる利
上げを行うことには十分な合理性が存在すると考える。
その上で、利上げの理由が金融緩和度合いの調整だけに回
帰する以上、日銀は、追加利上げをしても、依然として緩和的
な金融環境が維持されることを 、従来以上に丁寧に説明し 、
企業や家計、金融市場の納得感を得ることが求められる。
この点に関して 、日銀執行部のレポート （2026-J-5）は、金融
環境の評価軸を金利動向と資金調達動向の二つに整理し 、
前者に関しては名目と実質の双方の金利動向、 後者に関し
ては資金調達コストやアベイラビリティ 、資産価格、資金調達
量を例示している 。また、日本と同じく銀行中心の金融システ

ムの下にある欧州中央銀行の政策反応関数を踏まえると 、
銀行貸出を取り巻く環境（ 預貸利ザヤ、貸出姿勢や借入需要、
担保価値）なども含めることが考えられる。
これらの要素に関する指標や分析の多くは、 既に 「展望レ
ポート」の中に盛り込まれているとはいえ 、様々に分散してい
る面もある 。金融緩和度合いの判断をより強くアピールする
上では、声明文の付属資料として一覧性のあるかたちで示す
といった工夫も有用となる。

3月以降、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油高がインフレ懸念を高
め、消費者マインドを下押ししてきた 。6月17日に米国とイランが

い。雇用者数や低所得層の消費には弱さもあるが、 新規失業保
険申請件数や失業率は低位にあり 、求人倍率は1 倍台を回復し

ている。実質GDP成長率は1-3月期に前期比年率2.1％と潜在成
長率を上回ったが、 4-6月期は在庫積み増しもあって一段の加
速が見込まれる 。背景には、①AI関連設備投資の活況、 ②株高
による資産効果 、③大型減税・歳出法の効果 、④対米直接投資
の流入などがある。
物価をみると、コアPCEデフレーターは再加速している（図表1）。ト
ランプ関税や中東情勢に伴う原油高がコア財価格を押し上げたた
めだ。ただし、関税の影響は一巡しつつあるほか、原油高の影響
も年内のピークアウトが見込まれる。長期期待インフレ率（インフレ
スワップ5年先5年など）は原油価格の変動にもかかわらず安定し
ており、賃金やサービス物価への二次的波及の兆候は見えない。
もっとも、当社はインフレ率が来年以降も2％目標に着地しないと
みている。堅調な景気と需給ギャップの拡大がディマンドプル・ イ
ンフレを招くためだ 。コアPCEデフレータは年内は 3％台、来年も
2％台半ばでの高止まりを想定する 。この前提の下、金融政策は

年内は据え置き、来年は2回（6・12月）の利上げを見込む。一方、
金利先物市場は年内に1.4回の利上げを織り込んでいる 。FRBの
ウォーシュ議長がインフレ抑制を重視していることから、市場は将
来の物価上振れを回避する予防的利上げを想定している模様だ 。
仮に、原油価格の下落などで早期利上げ観測が後退すれば、 金
融市場では金利低下・ドル安・株高が進む可能性がある。
米長期金利は現在4 .5％近辺で高止まりしている （図表2）。昨年
来低下してきた期待政策金利（ 政策金利予想）は、イラン情勢に
伴うインフレ懸念や米景気の堅調、 FRBのタカ派化などを背景に
上昇に転じた。一方、高止まりしていたタームプレミアム （国債保
有リスク ）は和平交渉を受けて足元では低下している 。だが、中
間選挙に向けて拡張財政が加速すれば、 再び上昇しやすい 。さ
らに、ウォーシュFRB議長が量的引き締め （QT）を進める可能性
もある。不確実性や国債需給の悪化懸念は、長期金利の変動幅
を拡大させるとともに、上昇リスクを高めよう。

一方、株価は上昇継続とみる。AI・半導体株には過熱感も指摘さ
れるが、足元までの株高は企業業績（ EPS）の拡大が主因であり 、
市場心理（PER）の高揚だけによるものではない（図表3）。短期的
には株価急落はあり得るが、業績の裏付けがある分、大きく崩れ
るリスクは低いだろう。当社はAIの進展による生産性向上がEPS
を押し上げ、成長期待がPERを支えることで、年末のS&P500指数
は8000ポイント超を見込む。リスクは長期金利の上振れだ 。それ
はPERだけでなく、投資抑制を通じてEPSも押し下げる。当面はAI
期待の持続性と金利上昇リスクが株価の方向性を左右しよう。
実体経済は堅調、原油下落などで早期利上げ期待が後退すれば債券高・株高も
長期金利はインフレ懸念と国債需給懸念から上昇の公算、株式市場はAI期待が牽引

日本経済見通し
4
6月の日本の長期金利は変動の大きい展開となったが、 月末値

は月初値からほぼ横ばいとなった （図表1）。6月初めの長期金利
は植田日銀総裁の講演におけるタカ派的発言が意識されて上昇
した。中旬になると 、米国とイランの和平協議進展や日銀6 月会
合での国債買入れ減額停止観測を受けて、長期金利は下落に転
じた。その後、長期金利は再び上昇し始めた。米国の利上げ観測
が高まったことに加え 、6月会合の内田副総裁会見などからビハ
インドザカーブ懸念が再び意識されたことが挙げられる。
6月会合で日本銀行は今後のインフレリスクが大きいとの認識を
示したが、一方で利上げペースの前倒しや利上げ幅拡大の可能
性は示唆しなかった。債券市場では日銀のビハインドザカーブ懸
念が高まっているものの、 日銀は2026年度後半から27年度での
物価目標達成を予想し、強い懸念を示していない。中東情勢が最
悪期を過ぎたとみられることや、長期インフレ予想が2％に完全に
アンカーされたわけではなく上振れだけでなく下振れリスクもある
こと、今回の利上げで政策金利水準が中立金利の推計値下限付

近に到達したことなどから 、日本銀行は次回利上げのタイミング
を慎重に見極めていくと予想される。
基調的物価を見通すことは困難な状況だ 。中東情勢の先行きは
依然不透明である点や、仮に和平協議が進んだとしても、ホルム
ズ海峡の完全な正常化には時間を要すると予想されること、石油
製品の代替調達やサプライチェーンの変更に伴いコスト高は続く
こと、円安地合いが強まっていることなどから 、インフレが予想よ
りも長引くリスクは否定できない。一方、高市政権による補助金政
策によりコア CPIの押し下げ効果が続くことや、 来月にはCPIの5
年に1度の基準改定により過去分の遡及改定が予定されている
こと、やや先の話になるが食品の消費税率引き下げやそれに伴
う外食などへの補助金がCPIを下押しするとみられることなどから、
インフレの基調が読みにくくなり 、加えてコアCPI実績値の下振れ
を受けてインフレ予想も低下するという下方リスクもある。こうした
点も踏まえ 、日本銀行は金融環境の状況を見極めながら 、次の

利上げ時期を慎重に探っていく姿勢とみられる。当社メインシナリ
オでは引き続き次の利上げ時期を12月と予想する。ただし、為替
レートが170円/ドルに近づくような急速な円安が起これば 、利上
げ時期が前倒しされるリスクがある点には留意されたい。
日本の景気下振れリスクは低下している。石油備蓄日数をみると、
6月はわずか▲4日分の減少と、5月（▲6日分）に続いて小幅にと
どまった。もちろんミクロでみれば供給制約が依然厳しい分野も
あると推察されるが、 石油枯渇による景気後退という最悪のリス
クは後退したとみられる 。日銀短観6
月調査では、企業の景況感
は予想以上の改善、設備投資計画にも大きな変調はみられない 。
企業の値上げ意向は引き続き高く（ 図表2）、資金繰りなどを見る
限り、金融環境も引き続き緩和的だ（図表3）。4～5月の日銀消費
活動指数も高い伸び率であり 、特にサービス消費の増加が続い
ている点は明るい材料だ。株高などの資産効果もあり、推し活・イ
ベント需要などは引き続き旺盛とみられる。

再び高まる市場でのビハインドザカーブ懸念、一方で日銀は次回利上げ時期を見極めへ
日銀短観6月調査は強い結果、日本の景気後退リスクは低下

■図表1：ユーロ圏GDPは特殊要因を除いても弱含み
出所：EUROSTAT、SFGI
■図表2：ECBはインフレの顕在化に対応し、6月に利上げ
注：物価予想（点線部分）はECBスタッフが6月理事会で示した4つのシナリオ
のうちの「基本」と「穏健」。「逆風」「深刻」は割愛
出所：EUROSTAT、ECB、SFGI
■図表3：長期インフレ期待は引き続き安定
注：市場の長期インフレ期待は、インフレスワップ5年先5年
出所： ECB（Survey of Professional Forecaster)、Bloomberg、SFGI
ユーロ圏経済は低迷している 。2026年1-3月期の実質GDP 成長
率は前期比年率▲ 0.9％となった 。その主因は多国籍企業の活
動に左右されやすいアイルランドの特殊要因だが 、これを除いて
も1％近辺の成長にとどまっている （図表1）。国別ではドイツが相
対的に堅調で 、防衛費拡大やインフラ投資を中心とした財政支
出の増加が下支えとなった 。イタリア、スペインなどの南欧経済

は、人口増や観光ブームが成長を押し上げている 。一方、フラン
スでは政治的不確実性の高まりを背景に家計・ 企業マインドや
内需が弱含み、成長率はマイナス圏に沈んだ 。需要面では設備
投資が高金利環境の下で慎重化し、米国向けを中心に輸出も低
迷している。生産面では、イラン情勢に伴うエネルギー価格の上
昇がサービス業の重荷となっている 。先行きも停滞色の強い展
開が見込まれる。米国につられた域内の高金利が企業の投資を
抑制することや 、ユーロ高による輸出競争力の低下 、中国の需
要低迷などの逆風がある。また、貸出態度の厳格化や資金需要
の減少といった金融環境の引き締まりが警戒されるほか、 各地
を襲う熱波が生産 ・消費活動を下押しする反面 、食料や電力価
格を押し上げる可能性にも留意が必要だ。
金融政策では、物価上昇を背景に、ECBは6月理事会で約3年ぶ
りの利上げを決め 、預金ファシリティ金利を2 .25％へ引き上げた
（図表2）。ECBは今回の利上げについて 、将来のインフレリスク
に対する 「予防的」「保険的」な措置ではなく、 顕在化しているエ

ネルギーショックへの対応であることを強調した 。同時に発表し
た経済・ 物価見通しでは、 基本・穏健・逆風・深刻の4 つのシナリ
オを提示しつつ、来年以降の物価が2％目標を下回る穏健シナリ
オの下でも今回の利上げが正当化されることを強調した 。もっと
も、6月のインフレ率は5月よりも低下し、穏健シナリオの想定をも
下回った。また、ラガルドECB総裁も認めるように 、長期期待イン
フレ率は、2％物価目標と整合的な水準で安定している （図表3）。
現時点では、エネルギー価格上昇が賃金とサービス価格の持続
的な上昇につながる 、いわゆる二次的波及は限定的とみられる 。
そのためECBは、連続利上げを前提とした機械的な政策運営を
否定し、会合ごとに公表されるデータを評価しながら政策を判断
する方針だ 。また、6月末にシントラで開催された ECBフォーラム
においてラガルド総裁は 、不確実性が高い環境下ではあらかじ
め決められた金利経路は存在しないと改めて強調し 、フォワード
ガイダンスについても 、低インフレ時代に有効な政策手段であり 、

今後はこれに依存しない政策運営を志向する考えを示した。
なお、現在の預金ファシリティ金利2 .25％は、ECBが推計する中
立金利レンジ（1.75～2.25％）の上限に位置する。ここからの追加
利上げは実質的に引き締め領域への踏み込みを意味する。市場
では年内にあと1回弱の利上げが織り込まれているが、当社はエ
ネルギーインフレの年内ピークアウトと来年中の2 ％目標への収
束を想定し、政策は据え置きと予想する。経済成長率の低迷に加
え、金融環境が引き締まるリスクも踏まえ、金融政策は景気の下
振れや信用収縮のリスクへの配慮が必要な局面にあるといえる 。
ユーロ圏景気は低迷、先行きは金融環境の変化や熱波の影響に警戒
ECBは利上げを決定、インフレやインフレ期待は安定、金融政策は当面据え置きの公算

■図表1：6月の中国株は香港・本土市場で明暗が分かれる
出所：Bloomberg
■図表2：小売売上高はコロナ禍を除けば記録的な低水準
出所：中国国家統計局、Macrobond
■図表3：ハイテク輸出が景気のけん引役に
出所：中国海関総署、Macrobond

91 92 94 95 97 98 00 02 03 05 06 08 10 11 13 14 16 17 19 21 22 24 25
（年）
小売売上高
（前年比％）
中国経済見通し
6
6月の中国株のうち 、香港ハンセン中国企業指数は大幅下落、
CSI300指数は小幅に上昇した （図表1）。中東情勢の改善はプラ
ス要因となったものの、香港市場では内需悪化への懸念や当局
による違法な越境証券取引の取り締まり強化が嫌気された 。一

方、本土市場では政府による AIデータセンター建設への大型投
資計画や最先端技術開発加速方針が好感された。
中国経済は現在、輸出好調の一方で内需低迷という大きな構造
的矛盾に直面している 。5月の小売売上高はゼロコロナ政策解
除直後の2022 年12月以来初めて前年割れ （図表2）となり、不動
産不況を背景に固定資産投資も減少幅が拡大した 。一方で、半
導体などのハイテク品やEV、再生可能エネルギー関連製品を中
心に輸出は好調（図表3）で景気を支えている。こうした輸出主導
の成長により政府が掲げる5％前後の経済成長目標は達成可能
とみられるものの、欧米との貿易摩擦は一段と強まりつつある。
輸出を牽引しているのは、 中国政府が重点分野としている ハイ
テク品目だ。AI、半導体、ロボットなどへの巨額投資が実を結び 、
中国企業は世界市場で急速に存在感を高めている 。半導体輸
出額は前年同月比で 2倍超となり 、AI投資ブームを背景にコン
ピューター・データ処理機器などの輸出も大幅に拡大した 。また、

AI分野ではDeepSeek に続き、Zhipu（智譜）が高性能かつ低コス
トの大規模言語モデルを発表し 、中国AIは性能・ 価格の両面で
米国勢との差を縮めつつあるとの見方も広がっている 。ただし、
最先端半導体の不足や計算資源の制約から 、総合的には 依然
として米国AIが優位との評価も残る。
しかし、中国の新たな成長モデルには大きな課題もある 。ハイテ
ク産業は北京、上海、深セン、杭州など一部都市に集中しており 、
省力化が進むため雇用創出効果は従来の不動産や製造業ほど
大きくない 。その結果、 多くの地方都市では人口流出や住宅価
格下落、税収減少が続き 、若年層の雇用環境も改善していない 。
地方政府はAI パークや EV 工場の建設を競うものの、 サプライ
チェーンや人材不足から失敗例も多く、空き施設や稼働停止した
工場も目立つ 。さらに 、補助金競争が企業の過剰参入を招き 、
価格競争の激化によって企業収益は悪化し、赤字企業比率は過
去最高水準に達している 。こうした 産業政策が生産性向上を促

す一方で、資源配分の歪みも生み出している面もある。
こうした輸出依存の強まりは、 中国の国際的な立場にも影響を
与えている 。欧州では中国製品への依存を減らすデリスキング
の動きが広がり 、米国もAIや半導体だけでなく EV、クラウド 、ロ
ボット企業まで軍事関連企業リストを拡大した 。これに対し、中国
もレアアース などを通じた対抗措置を打ち出し 、米中対立は技
術・安全保障分野まで 一段と広がっている 。中国の 技術レベル
は着実に向上する一方で 、消費低迷、不動産不況、地方財政悪
化、地域格差という構造問題は依然として解決されていない 。製
造業や輸出への過度な依存から脱却し 、家計消費を中心とした
内需主導型の経済へ転換 すべきだが、 米国との地政学的な競
争における勝利を最優先する政策姿勢が続くと筆者は予想する 。
不振極まる中国の内需、輸出主導の成長でカバーする構図が継続
ハイテク産業振興政策は奏功、ただし格差などの深刻な構造問題を助長

輸送 旅行
保険・年金 金融
デジタル赤字 その他
サービス収支
（兆円）
6月30日、ドル円は約39年ぶりとなる162円台後半の高値を付
けた。2024年の高値161円95銭を上抜けると 、ドル高・円安が
加速。ここまで来ると 、テクニカル上あまり明確な上値抵抗線
はなく、強いて言えば 1986年11月に付けた戻り高値の 164円
50銭付近が、次の上値メドとして挙げられよう 。しかし、そこも
超えると、1985年のプラザ合意以降1 ドル＝240円台から急落
してきた相場なだけに 、上値メドを予想するのは難しく、 所謂
「青天井」になりかねない環境と言えよう。
今年4月～5月に実施された円買い介入は、 約12兆円の投入
で、ドル円の押し下げ幅は約5 円だったことから 「介入は効か
ない」との見方が専らだが、 むしろよく現在の水準に留まって

いるというのが筆者の印象だ 。161円95銭を超えて 、164円付
近まで一気に円安が加速してもおかしくない環境だが 、介入
警戒感がそれを阻んでいると思われる 。また、4月は直前まで
片山大臣が 「断固たる措置 」を連呼し 、連休中の介入も匂わ
せた上で実施された 。投機筋に円売りに対する不安感を与え
るためには 、前回のように明快な手法ではなく 、覆面介入に
よってじわじわと相場に影響を与えようとするかもしれない。
しかし、介入が時間稼ぎになったとしても、円安のトレンドはな
ぜ止まらないのか。 短期的には、米国の利上げ観測の高まり
によるドル高に加え、高市政権が6月30日に公表した「骨太の
方針」の原案が影響しているようだ 。同原案に、「『強い経済』
の実現を目指しており 、そのためにも『安定的な物価上昇』 の
実現に資する適切な金融政策運営を伴うことが非常に重要 」
との記載があったことが注目された 。前段の文脈を踏まえれ
ば政府としては 「デフレに後戻りすることのない物価安定 」を

目指しているのであって、これを「日銀の利上げに対するけん
制」だと捉えるのは 、やや過剰反応と言えるが 、市場ではそ
のように解釈されている 。加えて 、今回の「骨太の方針 」は、
「強い経済の実現」のための経済運営の政策ばかりが目立ち 、
財政改革の方針は見当たらず 、「財政健全化」 の文言も消え
た。これにより、財政懸念が再び台頭し 、市場では円安ととも
に長期金利が再び上昇しはじめている 。株価もさらに下落す
れば、「トリプル安 」となるリスクを孕む 。一方、長期的な円安
要因としては、 日本の潜在成長率が低いことに加え 、実質金
利が依然としてマイナスであることから 、投資マネーが日本か
ら海外へ流出しやすいことが挙げられよう 。財務省の金融収
支を見ると、今年4月時点の海外への直接投資は2 .8兆円と、
この20年で約7倍となっている 。円安進行により 、製造業の生
産は今後一部国内に戻る可能性はあるものの、 海外に向か
う日本の投資マネーを本格的に国内回帰させる 、或いは海外

からの投資が積極的に日本に向かうことを促すには、 日本の
成長期待が高まることが大前提だ。
ところで、筆者がやや違和感を覚える のは、「デジタル赤字が
円安を促している 」という説が 、最近報じられるケースが目立
つことだ。そもそも日本の経常収支は今年 4月時点で4.2兆円
の黒字で、所謂「デジタル赤字」 はそのうちのサービス収支に
含まれる 「通信・ コンピュータ ・情報サービス 、著作権等使用
料、コンサルティング料 」などに該当し 、約6,000億円と規模も
小さい。サービス収支の中身を詳しく見てみると （図表）、確か
にデジタル赤字（ 図表・ 黄色） は拡大しつつあるものの、 イン
バウンド効果による旅行収支の黒字（ オレンジ）の拡大で、ほ
ぼ相殺される規模である 。この他、ここ数年サービス収支の
赤字を拡大させている要因として 、保険・ 年金サービス （図
表・緑）が挙げられよう 。地震、台風、豪雨などの自然災害の
増加や大型化に伴い、日本の保険会社は海外の再保険会社
にリスクを移転する傾向にあり 、これがサービスの輸入増を

促している。また、保険会社による海外資産の運用の増加は 、
海外の金融機関による 資産管理や運用委託などのサービス
の輸入増に繋がっているようだ 。AIなどの最新テクノロジーが
米国のサービスに集中していることは言うまでもないが 、日本
のIT・デジタル化の遅れを踏まえれば、 今後も利用料などデ
ジタル赤字は増加傾向となり 、いずれ無視できない規模とな
る可能性はある。しかし、これまでの円安を主にデジタル赤字
で説明するのは、やや無理があるのではないか。

日銀、25bp利上げ
（6/16）
4月末、本邦政府・日銀による円買い・ドル売り介入によってド
ル円相場は 5月6日に2月24日以来の水準となる 155円ちょう
ど付近まで値を下げた。しかしその後はジリジリと値を戻し 、6
月18日には4月末の高値を抜けて上昇が加速 。7月1日には
1986年以来となる162円80銭を付けた。その後は日本の介入
への警戒感が燻る中で 、本稿執筆時点にかけては上値が抑
えられているが、積極的に円が買われる様子もまた乏しい。

こうしたドル円の上昇の主たる原動力は米ドル高だ。6月の米
連邦公開市場委員会（ FOMC）で示された経済見通し （SEP）
においては 、経済は潜在成長率をやや上回って推移する一
方、物価は上方修正され、 2028年にかけて高止まりするとの
見方が示された（詳細は6/18Inoue Report）。政策金利に関し
ては、FF金利の予想中央値は2026年末時点で3.75％と「据え
置き」 が示唆されたが、 ドットチャートは据え置きと利下げ予
想が計9 人、1回以上の利上げ予想が9 人と割れている状態
であり、市場はこのイベントを 「タカ派的」と評価した 。その後
は図表1・2の通り、年内の利上げ観測を強めつつ 、ドルが買
われる展開となった 。しかし、ドットチャートの分布を見るに 、
FOMCメンバーの見方のバラツキは大きいと言わざるを得な
い。また 、新たに米連邦準備理事会（ FRB）議長に就任した
ウォーシュ氏は本見通しに参加していない 。そのウォーシュ
氏は7月1日、現在の物価水準は「 依然として高すぎる 」という

認識をFRB 内で共有しているとしつつも 、中東に絡む混乱が
一定程度落ち着き 、原油価格が下がったことで 「（ここ数週間
で）インフレリスクは低下した 」と言及しており 、利上げには慎
重な様子も見受けられる 。一方、 市場は本稿執筆時点で年
末までに約1.4回程度の利上げを完全に織り込んでいる 。当
面は、この市場の織り込みが整合的か、 データを見ていく流
れとなろう。7月28‐29日のFOMCでの政策変更期待は薄いが、
現状をFOMCがどう認識しているのかが注目されよう 。弱い
経済指標が続くことがあれば 、市場が利上げ織り込みを修正
する中で強めのドル売り圧力が生じる可能性もある 。仮に
2022年・2024年時のように 、日本の円買い介入が弱い米指
標発表のタイミングに合わせて実施されれば 、ドル円相場の
水準が短期的に大きく下方へ修正されることも起こり得る。
ただし 、日本サイドの材料として 、6月末に示された政府の
「骨太の方針（原案）」を受け 、日銀の利上げが阻害されるの

では、財政健全化が後退するのでは、 との不安が広がってお
り、これが円の重石となっている。短期的なドル円の下押しが
あったとしても、じわじわと再び円売り圧力が強まる可能性は
留意すべきだろう 。米利上げについての市場の過度な織り込
みも、一旦大きな修正が入れば、再び金利先高感の中でドル
買い優勢に転じると見る 。当社は図表3のように、先月の当レ
ポートで示した見通しを維持する。
米国の金利先高感が米ドル上昇の原動力に
市場の利上げ織り込みの修正があれば短期的なドル安も

実績値 Baseline Milder Adverse Severe
(%)
出所：ECB、Macrobond
6月中旬以降 、ユーロは対ドルで上値の重い展開となってい
る。米経済の底堅さや、 6月米連邦公開市場委員会 （FOMC）
がタカ派的との受け止め方が広がる中 、為替市場において
はドル買いが優勢に 。ユーロドルは昨年6 月以来の安値水準
まで下落した 。一方、 対円では日本の財政悪化への懸念が
円の重石となる中、高値圏での推移が続いている（図表1）。
欧州中銀（ ECB）は6月11日の理事会で 、25bpの利上げを決
定した。今回注目されたのは 、ラガルド総裁が質疑応答で示
した判断の強さだ 。同総裁は、 今回の25bp 利上げは全会一
致で決定され、 他の選択肢は議論されなかったと発言。 また、
今回の決定が 「保険的・予防的」利上げとの見方を明確に否

定し、長期化するエネルギーショックが経済全体へ広がり始
めていることを踏まえた 、通常の金融政策判断だったと強調
した。さらに、従来のadverse、severeシナリオに加え 、エネル
ギー価格の上振れが軽微にとどまる milderシナリオを新たに
用意したうえで （図表2）、どのシナリオでも利上げは正当化さ
れると述べた点は 、ECBが単なるリスク管理ではなく 、見通し
に基づく政策判断として利上げを位置付けていることを示して
いる。なお、ラガルド総裁は後日ポルトガルのシントラで行わ
れたECBフォーラムにおいても、改めて同様の見方を示した。
もっとも 、今回の利上げはやや時期尚早感もある 。ラガルド
総裁は、5月のサービスインフレ率が+ 3.5％へ上昇したことに
ついて「大幅な上昇」と認めつつも、それがエネルギー価格の
直接・間接的影響によるものなのか、 あるいは別の要因なの
かはまだ見極めが必要とした 。また、賃金を通じた本格的な
二次的波及は現時点で確認されていないとも述べている 。6

月のユーロ圏HICP は前年比+2.8％と、5月の+3.2％から低下。
エネルギー価格はなお+8.7％上昇と高いものの 、サービス価
格は+3.2％へ鈍化し 、食品・ エネルギー等を除くコア指数も
+2.4％へ低下した。加えて、ECBの5月消費者期待調査では、
1年先の期待インフレ率は+3.5％へ低下し、3年先は+2.9％、5
年先は+2.4％で横ばいとなった。milderシナリオの追加を含め、
今回の決定は「利上げありき」のようにも見える。
また、景気面ではユーロの下押し材料も多い 。ECBの6月ス
タッフ見通しでは 、2026年と2027年の成長率が下方修正され
た。6月の総合PMI は50.0まで持ち直したものの、 これは内需
の持ち直しや中東情勢を巡る供給懸念の後退を要因とした
スペインの急回復に牽引されたという面が大きく、 ユーロ圏景
気全体の本格回復を示すものとまでは言い難い （図表3）。今
回の利上げにより 、政策金利は中立金利の推計レンジの上
限付近に位置していると考えられるが、 景気の力強さを欠く

中での高金利は、 企業の設備投資や資金需要の抑制を通じ
て、ユーロ圏経済の重石となる。こうした景気下押し圧力が意
識されれば、 市場で過度に織り込まれている追加利上げ期
待は後退し、対ドルを中心にユーロ売り圧力が強まるとみる。
利上げに踏み切るECB
ユーロ圏経済への影響は

森本 淳太郎
金融市場調査部 シニアアナリスト

### Page 12

2026年7月号
6月中旬以降、ポンドは対ドルで上値の重い展開となっている 。
ドルの強さを背景に 、一時は1.31ドル台まで下落する場面も
見られたが、 その後は英国の財政悪化懸念がやや後退した
ことなども背景に（後述）、持ち直す様子も見られている。また、
対円では、円の弱さが目立つ中で堅調地合いが続いており 、
4月30日の高値を更新している（図表1）。
英国では、スターマー首相の退陣表明を受け 、バーナム前マ
ンチェスター市長が次期首相に就任するとの見方が優勢だ 。
当初は、同氏の下で減税や歳出拡大が進み、 財政悪化懸念
が強まるとの警戒も見られた 。もっとも 、同氏は現行の財政
ルールを遵守する方針を示し 、市場の懸念払拭に努めてい
る。英国では2022年のトラス・ショックの記憶がなお新しく、 債
券市場の警戒感も根強い 。こうした中、新政権が市場の信認
を大きく損なうような極端に財政拡張的な政策を打ち出す可
能性は低く、 実際、超長期国債利回りが急騰する兆しも見ら
れていない（図表2）。バーナム氏が財政規律への配慮を示し

ていることは、ポンド相場に一定の安定感を与えている。
一方、英中銀（BOE）は6月18日の金融政策委員会 （MPC）で、
政策金利を3.75％に据え置いた 。決定は7 対2で、2名が25bp
の利上げを主張した 。BOEは、中東情勢を受けたエネルギー
価格上昇がインフレに与える影響を警戒しつつも 、労働市場
の軟化や景気減速が物価上昇圧力を抑制する可能性もある
として、今回は据え置きを選択した 。もっとも、英国のCPIは5
月時点で前年比+ 2.8％とBOE目標を上回り 、サービスインフ
レもなお高止まりしている 。企業の価格設定見通しも下げ
渋っており、BOEが直ちに利下げへ転じる可能性は低い。
ただ、英経済を見るとポンド売り材料も目立つ 。6月の英国総
合PMIは49.3と、5月の49.7から低下し 、2025年4月以来の低
水準となった（図表3）。サービス業PMI は一段と悪化し 、製造
業PMIも拡大圏ながら伸びは鈍化している 。また、賃金上昇
率は一時期に比べれば鈍化しており 、求人件数も 2021年初

以来の低水準まで減少した 。失業率は4.9％へ小幅に低下し
たものの、労働市場の基調としては緩和方向にある。
こうした中 、政策金利3.75％は、3％前後とされる中立金利を
大きく上回っており 、英国経済にとってかなり引き締め的な水
準だ。住宅ローンや企業向け融資の金利負担はなお重く、 設
備投資や資金需要を抑制することで 、景気下押し圧力を強め
る公算が大きい。市場では年内1
回程度の利上げがなお織り
込まれているが 、景気悪化が一段と鮮明になれば 、こうした
追加利上げ期待は後退しやすい 。バーナム新政権を巡る財
政不安は一旦落ち着きつつあるものの、 裏を返せば大規模
な財政支出による景気下支えは期待しにくい 。金融引き締め
による景気悪化が意識されれば、 対ドルを中心にポンド売り
圧力は強まるとみる。
英財政悪化への過度な懸念は後退
引き締め的な金融環境が英経済の重石に
為替相場見通し 英ポンド

政策金利
ＲＢＡ見通し（5月時点）
（％）
5月中旬以降 、豪ドルは対ドルで下落基調を辿っている一方、
対円ではやや弱いながらも 111円台では底堅さも見られる 。
主な要因は米国の金利先高観を背景とする米ドル高が続い
ていることであり 、ほとんどの通貨が対ドルで下落している中
で豪ドルも軟調になっている 。だがそれだけではなく、 豪ドル
市場の独自要因が逆風になっていると見られる。
豪中銀（ RBA）は2・3・5月の3つの理事会において 、連続で
25bpの利上げを決定。この時、RBAは「足元の金利水準はや
や緊縮的であり 、今後は経済情勢を見守る余地がある 」とす
る一方、 発表した経済見通しにおいては 、年内にあと 2回の
追加利上げの可能性が示唆されており （図表2）、インフレ警
戒度合いが非常に高い様子が示された 。金利先物市場は5

月理事会の翌週 、年内にさらに1.6回の追加利上げを完全に
織り込んでいた （図表3 ）。しかしその後発表された指標など
の結果を受けて 、豪州の追加利上げ織り込みを後退させる
流れとなった（本稿執筆時点では0.3回程度）。米国とイランの
停戦合意と、それに伴う原油安もこうした動きを促進したと見
られる。ただし、この市場の動きは、やや拙速なものである可
能性がある。
6月16日、RBA理事会は市場予想通り金融政策の据え置き
が決定した 。5月に予告されていた通り 、これまで3回実行さ
れた利上げの波及効果を見極める 、という趣旨であり 、見極
めた結果 、必要であれば追加利上げを行う 、というインフレ
ファイター的なスタンスは維持していた 。その後、6月24日に
発表された豪州の5月消費者物価指数（CPI）は、総合は市場
の伸び加速予想に反して減速したが、 RBAが重視する刈込
平均は前年比＋4.6％と、市場予想以上に伸びが加速。 目標
上限（3％）から上方に乖離した状態が続いている 。RBAのブ

ロック総裁は利上げが住宅価格下落などの需要抑制効果に
も寄与していることを指摘しつつも 、すでにインフレの二次的
波及の兆しが一部見られていることを指摘。 ハウザー副総裁
も6月24日、中東情勢の緊迫化が和らぐ中で原油安は歓迎さ
れるべきとしつつも 、「完全な解決はまだ保証されていない 。
豪国内のインフレ抑制に向け 、我々には まだやるべきことが
残っている」「インフレ率は依然としてあまりに高すぎる」として
いる。8月の経済見通し発表時にインフレ見通しや政策金利
見通しが下方修正される可能性はあるが、それでも金利先物
市場ほど見通しがハト派化していない場合、 市場が修正を迫
られることになる。その場合、豪ドルには上昇圧力が掛かろう 。
今後の経済データと市場の織り込みの変化には注目だ。
ただ、基本的には米ドル主導の相場である点には変わりはな
く、市場における米国の利上げ期待の推移と米ドルの動きが
最重要と言える。また、豪ドル円相場においては、 本邦政府・
日銀による円買い介入の有無も影響する点は留意したい。

為替相場見通し 豪ドル

グサテライト 」、日経CNBC などにレギュ
ラー出演し、金融市場の解説を行ってい

問。人生投資アカデミー® 学長。

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Market sentiment continued to deteriorate on Friday, with
the themes that dominated earlier in the week remaining in
focus as concerns around semiconductor valuations weighed
further on equities. Oil prices rose following reports the Trump

administration had notified Israel it was sending additional
refuelling aircraft to the country, a potential sign that US
military operations in the region could broaden.
Over the weekend, tensions in the Middle East escalated
further, with Iran’s deputy foreign minister declaring the
country had suspended its commitments under the interim
peace deal. With the MoU effectively abandoned, both sides
intensified strikes, including an Iranian attack on a US base

in Jordan that resulted in the deaths of two American service
members, while a third was killed in Iraq. In response, the US
expanded its operations to include non-military assets such
as bridges, utilities and ports, while Iran targeted a power and
water desalination plant in Kuwait.
• Equities fell on Friday as investors continued to rotate out
of AI-related investments amid concerns around elevated
valuations. Reflecting this shift, Apple once again became

the world’s largest company by market capitalisation,
overtaking Nvidia. In the US, the S&P 500 declined 1.0%,
led by a 1.4% fall in the NASDAQ, while the Dow Jones
lost 0.8%. The VIX rose 12% to 18.8, highlighting the
increase in market volatility. European equities were mixed,
with the Euro Stoxx 50 down 0.8%, while the FTSE 100
outperformed globally, rising 0.3%.
• Asian markets extended their losses into the end of the
week. The Nikkei 225 fell 4.0%, while the Hang Seng

snapped a five-day winning streak, declining 1.8%. Korean
markets were closed for the Constitution Day public holiday.
Locally, the ASX 200 fell 0.5%, ending the week lower
overall.
• US Treasuries were mixed, with the 2-year yield rising 4bps
while the 10-year yield declined 1bp. Futures markets
continue to price an additional Fed hike this year, with
December seen as the most likely timing, taking the implied
year-end cash rate to 3.94%.
• Australian government bond yields ended the week on

a stable footing, while reversing the previous session’s
moves. The 3-year yield rose 1bp, and the 10-year yield
was unchanged. Swap markets remain unconvinced of an
additional RBA hike, with the implied year-end terminal cash
rate at 4.51%.
• FX markets were relatively subdued on Friday, with the
USD broadly stable and the DXY little changed on the day.
The AUD weakened 0.2% to 0.6966, while the sterling also
fell 0.2% against the greenback. The euro and yen were

unchanged against the USD. Across the crosses, the AUD
declined 0.3% against the euro, and fell 0.2% against the
NZD and yen, while holding steady against sterling.
• Commodities saw oil prices continue to rise as markets priced
renewed escalation in the Middle East, with Brent recording
its strongest weekly gain since April, up almost 16% on last
Friday’s close. Brent rose 4.6% on Friday to US$88.1/bbl,
while WTI gained 4.5% to US$82.5/bbl. Gold recovered

1.0%, maintaining a foothold above US$4,000/oz. Copper
fell 0.5% and aluminium declined 1.1% as markets weighed
a weaker industrial outlook, while iron ore edged 0.1%
higher.
International Data
US University of Michigan consumer sentiment rose from
49.5 in June to 54.4 in July, still a very low level versus history.
Current conditions and expectations both improved in the
month. 1-year inflation expectations meanwhile eased from
4.6% to 4.2%, while 5-year expectations were unchanged at

3.3%yr.
US industrial production edged up 0.1% in June with
manufacturing activity unchanged.
US import prices rose 0.3% in June, markedly lower than May’s
1.7%, but materially above the -0.7% expectation for June. Ex
petroleum, price growth was largely as expected, up 0.5%.
US housing starts surged 19% in June after falling 15.2% in
May. Building permits fell 3.0% in June.
Local Data
On Friday, we published our June Labour Force preview ahead
of Thursday’s release, highlighting expectations for slower

employment growth and rising underemployment, suggesting
labour market slack is becoming more broadly based (see here).
Our New Zealand team also published their June quarter CPI
preview ahead of Tuesday’s release, with higher fuel prices
expected to lift annual inflation to its highest rate in two years
(see here).

## Document 344: The Institute Employment Report_ June 2026 Payroll growth strengthened in June as unemployment trends improved and lower-income wage growth accelerated. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Institute Employment Report_ June 2026 Payroll growth strengthened in June as unemployment trends improved and lower-income wage growth accelerated. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-07-01
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

12990925     1

Economy 
The Institute Employment Report: June 2026 
07 July 2026

Key takeaways 
• Bank of America customer deposit account data suggests labor market momentum improved again in June, with estimated 
payroll growth accelerating to 1.7% year-over-year (YoY). This strength appears broad-based, with job growth picking up in both 
FIFA World Cup 2026™ host cities and non-host cities, indicating the improvement extends beyond any tournament-related 
boost. 
• Consistent with a healthy labor market, unemployment payments into Bank of America customer accounts continued to soften 
in June. The number of households receiving unemployment benefits showed further moderation, reinforcing signs that labor 
market conditions remain resilient despite softer official payroll readings. 
• Lower-income households saw a rebound in after-tax wage growth, rising to 4.1% YoY in June, above middle-income households 
(3.4%) and nearly matching higher-income households (4.2%). Increased job switching activity, particularly among lower-income 
workers who tend to receive larger pay gains when changing employers, could be helping narrow the wage growth gap.

June job growth was solid in deposit account data 
Our estimate of job growth – which uses Bank of America deposit data to track changes in the number of customer accounts 
receiving a paycheck (see Methodology) – rose 1.7% year-over-year (YoY) in June. The data can be noisy, partly due to seasonal 
variation and differences in pay-period timing. Nonetheless, in our view, it suggests there is some continued momentum in the 
labor market. 
While official data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) showed that nonfarm payrolls rose only 57,000 in June, the YoY 
growth rate of this measure has also been rising since February (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: June payroll growth remained strong in Bank of America 
deposit account data

### Page 2

2 07 July 2026

I NSTI TUTE

Has the FIFA World Cup 2026™ been a significant driver of the acceleration in jobs growth? To analyze this, we look at jobs’ 
growth in our data for cities that are hosting games and those that are not. When we do this, it is not obvious there is a strong 
impact from the tournament: while job growth has accelerated more in 2026 relative to the second half of 2025 in host cities, 
those that aren’t hosts have also seen an acceleration (Exhibit 2).  
And consistent with an underlying continued improvement in the labor market, Bank of America data on unemployment 
payments into customer accounts shows easing growth (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Unemployment payment growth in June declined further in Bank of America consumer deposit data 
Number of households receiving unemployment payments (three-month moving average, YoY%, not seasonally adjusted (NSA)) and Continuing Claims (three-
month moving average, YoY%, seasonally adjusted (SA))

Source: Bank of America internal data, Bloomberg 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower-income households’ wage growth has picked up strongly  
Bank of America data continues to show signs of a significant recovery in lower- and middle-income households’ after-tax wage 
growth. In fact, lower-income households saw wage growth accelerate to 4.1% YoY in June, the highest rate since July 2023 and 
above the 3.4% YoY growth rate recorded by middle-income households. At the same time, higher-income households’ wage 
growth declined to 4.2% YoY in June – meaning their previous lead over lower-income households has largely disappeared.

### Page 3

07 July 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Will this improvement in lower- and (to a lesser extent) middle-income households’ after-tax wage growth persist? It is hard to 
be sure. One possibility is that some lower- and middle-income households have decided to reduce their tax withholdings this 
year to reflect changes from the One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBBA), thereby boosting their take-home pay growth. If this is the 
case, the impact on after-tax wage growth could last for a year.  
But the underlying improvement we have seen in job growth this year could also be part of the explanation. In Bank of America 
internal data, we see signs that households were moving between jobs at a faster rate in Q2 2026 than they were in Q2 2025 
(Exhibit 5). And while this is true across income cohorts, lower-income households have also been getting larger pay bumps of 
around 12%, compared to 9% for higher-income households, when they move jobs (for more on this, read our publication: Should 
I stay or should I go? The pay tradeoff). In this scenario, this effect may last as long as the improvement in the labor market 
continues but would be vulnerable to a pullback if labor demand slows.

4 07 July 2026

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be 
considered in the context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may 
provide directional and/or predictive value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized 
selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with 
a deposit account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small 
Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. 
Bank of America per Small Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit 
account or a Small Business credit card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include 
business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to 
periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following 
reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This 
could be due to an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America 
cards in the month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to 
underlying economic momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during 
COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data 
from Bank of America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever 
median household savings and checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a 
consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month 
of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only 
consumer card holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate 
cards are excluded. Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization 
Code (MCC) defined by financial services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the 
North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending 
data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to childcare, rent, insurance, public 
transportation, and tax payments. Discretionary services includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, 
entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-
December period is usually at least 20% of total annual spending on the category. 
For analysis looking at higher value transactions (including durables), we consider a value per transaction threshold estimated 
with reference to the top 30% of transactions by value in 2024. The share of higher value transactions is then the number of 
transactions above this threshold as a percentage of total transactions over time.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer 
deposit account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are 
bucketed according to terciles, with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower 
income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between 
these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of factors that impact income, including general wage inflation,

### Page 5

07 July 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The income and tercile in which a household is 
categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older 
Millennials: born between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include 
purchases of non-gas items. 
Estimate of payrolls growth from Bank of America internal data is based on the change in customer accounts receiving a 
paycheck in the month. An adjustment is made for the difference between overall population growth and customer account 
growth.  
An estimate of bonus growth from Bank of America deposit data is calculated by looking at customers who have received an 
inbound ACH payroll transaction in the last two years. From this sample an estimate of bonuses is derived by looking for payroll 
transactions which are over 50% higher than the median regular payroll payments received by the customer. Of these payments 
only those that were received around the same time in each of the last two years are selected.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 347: The rise of value Why have lower-income households accounted for a larger share of retail spending but also traded down the most_ 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/The rise of value Why have lower-income households accounted for a larger share of retail spending but also traded down the most_ 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-07-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

12994159     1

Economy 
The rise of value 
15 July 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumers are searching for value again, according to Bank of America internal card data. Spending at discount clothiers and 
value grocers started to accelerate again early this year. Price-conscious consumers are increasingly looking to general 
merchandise stores for deals and discounts. 
• Looking across the income distribution, higher-income households still punch well above their weight in retail spending but have 
made up a smaller share of spending at discount clothiers and value grocery stores, according to Bank of America internal data. 
Conversely, lower-income households account for a larger share of retail spending (excluding gas and autos), while also gaining 
share in the discount apparel and value grocers markets. 
• Additionally, lower-income families have demonstrated an interesting economic pattern, according to Bank of America internal 
data. Their wages have best kept pace with inflation compared to pre-pandemic levels, but they have also traded down the most 
- experiencing five times faster spending at discount apparel stores than higher-income households so far in 2026.

Consumers search for value again 
US consumers appear to be searching for value again as tariffs and elevated gasoline prices squeeze household budgets. Bank of 
America credit and debit card data suggests shoppers are returning to value grocers (see Methodology), with spending growth at 
these stores accelerating after cooling for nearly three years (Exhibit 1 and read more in Consumer Checkpoint: The madness of 
March).  
Exhibit 1: Spending growth at value grocers eased as gasoline prices declined, but has started to tick back up in the past few months 
Per household spending growth, based on Bank of America card data, by select categories (three-month moving average, year-over-year (YoY)%) compared to 
gasoline prices all grades all formulations (monthly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data, Energy Information Administration (EIA) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
It’s not just groceries where US consumers appear to be searching for value – discount apparel (see Methodology) has also seen 
a sharp increase in spending growth since April 2025. It’s possible that more consumers started heading to the discount tier to

seek less expensive clothing as “Liberation Day”-related tariffs drove prices up. Additionally, spending growth in discount apparel 
has eased since April 2026, likely because lower pre-tariff prices are no longer part of the YoY comparison. 
Another area attracting budget-conscious consumers may be general merchandise retailers (including big box stores and 
wholesalers). According to Bank of America credit and debit card data, spending growth at such stores hovered around 3% YoY 
from the beginning of 2024 before accelerating to just under 5% in June 2026 (Exhibit 2). Notably, spending has grown faster at 
general merchandise stores than overall retail (excluding gas and autos) over the same period.  
Exhibit 2: General merchandise has seen solid growth over the past two years

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Have general merchandise stores taken market share from other discount retailers?  
Since 2023, general merchandise stores have captured a much larger share of retail (excluding gas and auto) spending compared 
to pre-pandemic levels, according to Bank of America internal data (Exhibit 3). By contrast, the share of spending at value 
grocers has remained relatively flat, while discount apparel has seen a modest decline.    
In our view, this suggests that some retail spending may have shifted to general merchandise stores, potentially reflecting their 
broad offerings. Another factor may be the expansion of private-label (store-brand) products at big-box retailers and wholesalers 
across both grocery and apparel categories, according to BofA Global Research. Typically, these products are less expensive and 
designed to attract consumers looking to save.  
Exhibit 3: The share of retail (excluding gas and autos) spending at general merchandise stores has increased over the past four years and compared

However, Consumer Price Index (CPI) inflation data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) gives us another side to the story.
